1. SUTI Introduction

1.1 SUTI and its organization
SUTI is a Swedish abbreviation that translates to standardized exchange of transport information in a demand
responsive transport (DRT) context. To be short in English: Standardized Utilization of Transport Information.

We deliver an open standard for Clients that use one or several Providers of demand responsive transport (DRT)
and need a standard for this type of communication.

It is a two-way communication protocol containing messages sent between the Client and the Provider for different
business purposes.

In short you can say that the Client represents the demand of the travellers while the Provider represents the
Provider of transport services (vehicles, drivers etc.) satisfying the transport demands.

SUTI is widely adopted within public DRT in the Nordic countries. Communication of orders using the SUTI
standard is commonly a requirement in public procurements. More than 30 million demand responsive orders are
annually communicated using the SUTI prodocol.

The protocol is derived from a joint effort from the demand responsive industry and major users (Clients) in the
Nordic countries since 2002.

The standard is owned by the organisation SUTI, who is a member-owned non-profit organisation located in
Sweden with organisation-number 802412-3146. Please find list of members and more description on our home
page at suti.se.


1.2 Short summary of SUTI documents (The SUTI Standard)
The SUTI documentation can be found at suti.se and on GitHub at https://github.com/SUTI-se/SUTI.

Introduction to SUTI
This document gives a brief overview of the SUTI standard, suitable for beginners who are just getting started
implementing the SUTI standard. It can also be used as a presentation of the standard to non-developer roles.

How to use SUTI (this document)
The main document, that describes how SUTI can be used in for solving different situations. The document
assumes that the reader is familiar with the messages described in the Messages document.

Messages
The document gives a short definition of each type of message that exists in the SUTI standard. Each definition
will state who is the sender (Client or Provider) and what action the message is likely to trigger when received. A
deeper understanding of SUTI is best given by reading this document (How to use SUTI), while Messages is more
of a reference guide.

Schema (xsd)
The XML-schema that each message shall comply with. It also contains information regarding the use of certain
elements. Every implementation of SUTI shall comply with the schema and a validation of messages should leave
no errors.
For JSON there is also an equivalent schema in development. Currently, only the JSON version of the message
for communication of bulkLocations (i.e. repetitive updating of coordinates for all or engaged vehicles for a
Provider) is part of the official standard.

Enumeration list
All major enumerations for different SUTI-attributes, where SUTI have set a standard.

1.3 How to conform with the standard
SUTI provides the possibility for several different ways of configuring a connection between Client and Provider. A
simple taxi-app requires much less functionality than a complicated dynamic combination of trips for several
travellers with similar transportation demands. The latter is very common for public DRT.


                                                    Page 8 of 170
How to use SUTI

The use of SUTI is always a process of an agreement between the Client and its potential Providers.

Therefore, all Clients using SUTI shall make a Self declaration, stating how to use the standard when
communicating with them. The Technical Committee of SUTI (TC) can help advise in this process.

This document will cover rules and examples for how to set up a Self declaration. Once finished, you can submit it
to the TC, who will check that it will be reasonably simple for Providers to interpret and implement or guide you to
find alternatives if not. This service is available to members of SUTI.

The TC can also provide you with existing examples of Self declarations.

As Client you will have the following benefits from creating a Client Self Declaration:
   - You secure an understanding among your Providers of what you want to accomplish
   - You know that your description has a wide acceptance among your Providers
   - You minimize potential problems in your relations with Providers
   - You will make a one-time effort that will pay off very quickly
   - You can minimize the technical descriptions in your tenders
   - You know that you are likely to get offers with a lower amount of risk

Providers will normally not create Self declarations, but rather declare that they conform with the Self declaration
of a Client, typically in a public procurement tender.

Providers will normally be assisted by their traffic system vendors to check and verify that they can conform.




                                                     Page 9 of 170
How to use SUTI




2. SUTI Basics

2.1 SUTI terms and relations

2.1.1 Introduction
This chapter describes the most fundamental terms of the SUTI standard and the relations between these terms.
An in-depth description of terms in the standard is provided in chapter 5 Use of information elements.

Furthermore, the scope of the standard is restricted to Demand Responsive Transport (DRT).

The terms can either be found as elements in our XML representation, as attributes to elements or as descriptions
of elements and attributes.

2.1.2 Client, Provider, Agreement and Link
A Client needs transportation for its travellers. The transportation needs are normally collected in a booking and
planning system.

A Provider uses Vehicles to meet transportation needs that are communicated from the Client system. In DRT,
Providers normally use a traffic control system for monitoring the vehicles.

An Agreement is normally set up between the Client and one or more Providers to solve the transportation needs.
In public transport, such Agreements most often are the result of a public procurement process.

A Link is a SUTI communication set-up between a Client system and a Provider system, for a specific Agreement.

2.1.3 Message, Request, Response, Flow and Block

A Message is a fundamental term in SUTI. Using the SUTI protocol, the Client and Provider exchange Messages
to fulfill needs on each part.

A need is expressed as a Request in a Message. The other party will act on the Request with a Response in one
or more Messages.

For each type of need there will often be a chain of Messages. Such a sequence of Messages is described in
chapter 4 Use cases (Flows). The word Flow is used to describe a sequence of Messages.

Message Types (msgType) are identified using a four-digit number and a short name. Messages are grouped into
Blocks, where each Block contains Message Types within a certain domain. The first digit in the Message Type
number is the same for all Message Types in a Block.

The Message Types of the standard are divided into the following Blocks:

Block 1: General exchange of information normally related to Resources (see 2.1.10).

Blocks 2-6 (Order, Dispatch, Traffic Control, Communication and Report): handling of Orders (see 2.1.5).

Block 7: Technical control of the protocol.

Block 8: Accounting between Client and Provider.

2.1.4 Route, Node, Content, Traveller, Parcel, Pickup, Destination and Trip

For modelling the physical transportation domain, the following terms are used to describe what is transported,
and how the transport is carried out.


                                                    Page 10 of 170
How to use SUTI

Each transport has at least one Content. The dominating Content types are Traveller (sometimes called
Passenger) or Parcel, although there are a few additional very specific types in our enumeration list
SUTI_Enumeration_values.csv.

Content is transported along a Route in SUTI. A Route is a sequence of Nodes. A Node is not just a geographical
place; it also contains the Content and descriptions of all other requirements to solve the situation of a Node. Thus
a Route can be viewed as a sequence of activities.

Nodes describe activities in SUTI. Therefore, each Node has a Node type, where the most common types are
Pickup and Destination. A single Content usually has one Pickup Node and one Destination Node.

Since the SUTI standard is built around a sequence of Nodes with activities, such as picking up or dropping of
Content. The SUTI communication does not contain a word to describe the transport of a single Content. We
recommend using the term Trip to describe such a transport.

A Route can be very complex, containing many Nodes with activities for several different Contents.

2.1.5 Order, SubOrder, Repetetive Order, OrderTemplate, DriverSession and Tour

These terms all describe containers of a Route, that in turn contains a number of Nodes.

An Order consists of a Route. The Route can either be dynamically built up by additions of Nodes or being static.
Some Orders are built from more simple orders, called SubOrder.

A Repetitive Order is a plan for Orders over a period, e.g. School transports. The Repetitive Order is represented
as an OrderTemplate having a Calendar and scheduled Routes.

DriverSession consists of Orders that are dynamically added to a DriverSession. It is assumed that the Orders are
simple Orders (Base Order). Ideally these Orders should be Trips, however it is not explicitly required. No
references to SubOrders are allowed, as SUTI only allows two levels in the order hierarchy.

Tour is used for accounting between the parties of an Agreement. A Tour is a representation of several Nodes and
its Events (see below). A Tour may comprise several Orders performed in a sequence. If DriverSessions is used,
the Tour will most likely represent one DriverSession.

All these containers refer to an Agreement between parties for exchanging and acting on both parts.

2.1.6 Events

Events are the registration and communication of specific activities for each Node, like “Vehicle at Node”,
“Passenger in Vehicle”, etc. An event is normally registered manually by the Driver using the Vehicle equipment.
Not all types of equipment in Vehicles support all different Events specified in SUTI.

NOTE: For historical reasons and backward compatibility, Events are placed under the PickupConfirmation tag (as
it was the first Event type). The attribute EventType clarifies that there are many different Events under
PickupConfirmation.

2.1.7 Time

Time is a central concept in SUTI, as timing is crucial in most situations. SUTI handles four different TimeTypes:

- Scheduled generally refers to the planned time that has been agreed upon with the Traveller

- Estimated refers to the prognosticated time that may change based on real-time deviation from the plan

- Actual refers to the time an event actually occured

                                                    Page 11 of 170
How to use SUTI

- ASAP (As Soon As Possible) is used when it has been requested that something shall happen as soon as
possible.

This allows for sending Events in advance with an Estimated Time.

A Time can also define a DwellTime. A DwellTime is specified when additional time is required to perform some
activity at a Node, e.g. some form of assistance.

2.1.8 Address, Location and Geographic Location

To describe geographic properties, e.g. in a Node, the term Address is used. An Address can be expressed in
several ways, Geographic Location (coordinates) being one of them.

An Address can contain attributes like street, streetno etc. In this context, the attribute “location” refers to a
geographical area, e.g. a postal area or a taxiarea. This is an exception, as “location” in all other SUTI contexts
refers to geographical coordinates.

2.1.9 Content (Passenger, Parcel) Attributes, Connection

In addition to travelling along a Route, Content can have one or many Attributes that define restrictions for the
Transport depending on characteristics of the Content. Each type of Attribute is described in the SUTI Attribute
List. In a Self Declaration it is essential to describe the types of Attributes that may occur in an implementation.

A Connection is defined when a Node is dependent on a Connection with another Transport. Commonly this is
either a flight, a train or some other type of timetable bound transport.

2.1.10    Resource (Driver, Vehicle) Attributes

Vehicle and Driver are Resources, further described in chapter 5 Use of information elements. The Attributes of a
Content are matched with demands on either the Vehicle or the Driver. Thus, both of them have a long list of
matching Attributes, also described in the SUTI Attribute List.

2.1.11    Economy, Price, Taximeter, FormOfPayment, Cash, Account, SocialServiceFee

Economy in SUTI comprises mainly two parts: Price and formOfPayment.

Price allows for several types of price calculation. One type describes in generic terms how a Taximeter should be
set in order to measure the Price of a Transport.

FormOfPayment describes how a Transport is paid for. Among many options, the following three are common:

- Cash is used when the Passenger pays in the Vehicle (either by cash or card).

- Account is used when payment takes place outside car, in an invoicing transaction.

- SocialServiceFee is used when the Passenger should pay only part of the Price.

2.2 Terms out of the SUTI scope
2.2.1 Booking, Segment, Reservation
Booking is a term used in the travel industry when Reservations are made for different types of activities and
resources. The Reservations are normally gathered into a Passenger Name Record (PNR) having one common
description of payer and user (Passenger, Parcels).

A PNR can hold many Segments. Segments can be of different types like a flight or a taxi trip.

Normally these terms and processes are handled in a Client system and thus is out of the SUTI scope. SUTI can

                                                     Page 12 of 170
How to use SUTI

be used to ask a Provider to perform a taxi Segment. In such cases the Trip is transformed to an Order.

2.2.2 Fleet, Settlement (Dispatch)
A SUTI Provider normally Dispatches an Order to a Vehicle or a Fleet of Vehicles. This process is normally
handled in the Provider system and is also out of the SUTI scope.

However, there is an mechanism in SUTI for the Client to reject a Dispatch proposal. Block 3 in SUTI handles the
proposals and the results of the Dispatch process.

There is also an Order Status “dispatching”, meaning that the Provider is currently searching for a Vehicle to
accept the Order.

The Provider normally has a process named Settlement, for financial settlement between the Vehicle and the
Provider Company. This process is also internal to the Provider, and out of the SUTI scope. For the settlement
between the Client and Provider the term Accounting is used.

2.3 Data interchange formats (XML and JSON)
The first version of SUTI was based on eXtensible Markup Language (XML) as the data format. Between 2002
and 2020 XML was the only format used. From 2021 JSON has been introduced as a second data format, initially
used for transmission of Vehicle locations. In the documentation you will therefore almost exclusively find
examples in XML. Gradually, JSON examples will be introduced as well.

Any party that wishes to implement a SUTI Link using JSON are welcome to contact the SUTI Technical
Committee for guidance and support.

XML was selected due to the possibility to define a format specification, called XML Schema, that all SUTI
messages shall comply with. The XML Schema is a very powerful tool to secure compliance, and many modern
system development tools support validation of XML-files based on a given XML Schema.

The introduction of JSON examples in the documentation will be gradual, as JSON support is implemented for
additional Message Types.

2.4 Communication methods
The most efficient way to communicate between Client and Provider is to use a two-way communication, where
the Provider will act as server for requests from Client to Provider and the Client will act as server for requests
from Provider to Client.

Two terms that are avoided in SUTI are Service and API, as they normally do not express Message Flows the
same way as SUTI. Setting up a Service or API does not exclude the possibility of using SUTI structures.
However, SUTI Flows are normally two-way communication as opposed to regular client-server communication.
To implement such bidirectional communication in SUTI would therefore require the implementation of a Service or
API in both the Client and the Provider system.

Messages that are not supported by either the Client or Provider shall be handled using MSG 7030 Syntax Error or
MSG 7031 Not operational, to inform the calling party that a Message is invalid.

Currently three different methods of SUTI communication exist:

1. Two-way IP-protocol socket communication, using different ports for Client and Provider. To secure such links
   they are often embedded into VPN-communication. There are some advantages to this method. But there are
   often more disadvantages due to overhead in the VPN-protocol and difficulties with firewall configuration on
   both sides, when changing or setting up communication with many parties involved.

2. The Client providing an URL, that the Provider polls for incoming messages and delivers outgoing messages
   to. This method has the advantage that the Client can set up one URL that serves all Providers. The negative
   effect of polling is that it generates a large communication overhead.

3. Communication via WebSockets, which has the same advantages as two-way IP-communication but using a
   persistent connection, thus reducing overhead.

4. Bidirectional asynchronous HTTP Post: asynchronous communication using HTTP Post messages in both
                                                    Page 13 of 170
How to use SUTI

    directions is an extension of method 2 above, that uses only one URL on the Client side.

    This method of using HTTP Post enables a Provider to configure one single service that is common for all
    SUTI Links of the Provider side, significantly reducing the cost of establishing new links.

2.4.1 SUTI Communication using HTTP Post on each side
This chapter describes an example where method 4 in the previous chapter is used for communication.

In setting up a Link, the Client and Provider each establish a HTTPS Webservice that responds to HTTP Post
requests following these rules:
1. The request parameters senderSutiId, receiverSutiId and msgType are included to specify which
    SUTI Link the message belongs to and the Message Type to be expected in the body.
2. Basic Authorization is used.
3. Content-Type shall be either application/json; charset=UTF-8 or application/json;
    charset=UTF-8 depending on whether XML or JSON is used.
4. For increased security, each server shall use white-listing of the calling party allowing only authorized parties to
    use the service.

cURL example for a ”Keep Alive” JSON format

curl --location --request POST 'https://holmedal.se/v1?senderSutiId=systemprov_provsite_0001&receiverSutiId=
systemclient_clientsite_0009&msgType=7000' \
--header 'Content-Type: application/json; charset=UTF-8' \
--header 'Authorization: Basic aG9sbWVkYWw6QTFCMmMj' \
--data-raw '{"SUTI_7000_keepAlive": {"msg": {
                                                "msgType": "7000",
                                                "msgTimeStamp": "2019-12-18T10:39:00.000",
                                                "orgSender": {
                                                     "name": "ThisProvider",
                                                     "idOrg": {
                                                          "src": "SUTI:idLink",
                                                          "id": "systemprov_provsite_0001",
                                                          "unique": true}
                                                },
                                                "orgReceiver": {
                                                     "name": "ThisClient",
                                                     "idOrg": {
                                                          "src": "SUTI:idLink",
                                                          "id": "systemclient_clientsite_0009",
                                                          "unique": true}
                                                },
                                                "idMsg": {
                                                     "src": "systemprov_provsite_0001:MSGID",
                                                     "id": "2019121812345678",
                                                     "unique": true
                                                },
                                                "referencesTo": [{
                                                          "idMsg": {
                                                              "src": "systemclient_clientsite_0009:MSGID",
                                                              "id": "2019121887654321",
                                                              "unique": true}
                                                     }
                                                ]
                                            }
                                 }
                  }’

                                                         Page 14 of 170
How to use SUTI

cURL example ”Keep Alive” XML format

curl --location --
request POST 'https://holmedal.se/v1?senderSutiId=systemclient_clientsite_0009&receiverSutiId=
systemprov_provsite_0001&msgType=7000' \
--header 'Content-Type: application/xml; charset=UTF-8' \
--header 'Authorization: Basic aG9sbWVkYWw6QTFCMmMj' \
--data-raw '<SUTI>
  <orgSender name="ThisClient">
    <idOrg src="SUTI:idLink" id="systemclient_clientsite_0009" unique="true"/>
  </orgSender>
  <orgReceiver name="ThisProvider">
    <idOrg src="SUTI:idLink" id="systemprov_provsite_0001" unique="true"/>
  </orgReceiver>
  <msg msgType="7000" msgName="Keep alive">
    <idMsg src=" systemclient_clientsite_0009:idMsg" id="2015121414795234" unique="true"/>
  </msg>
</SUTI>'



2.5 GDPR and information security
SUTI communication may contain elements with personal data, e.g. passenger name, id, contact information,
attributes, etc. and driver information, that falls under GDPR.

It is the responsibility of the parties to secure that they either have a legitimate interest or consent from all
individuals whose personal data is being transferred over SUTI, and in all other ways act in accordance with
GDPR and any other relevant legislation. The use of personal data shall also be minimized to what is essential to
fulfilling the communication needs.

As an example, the SUTI protocol has been adapted to make it possible to minimize transfer of personal
information, i.e. by allowing calls between passenger and driver without revealing telephone numbers.

It is also strongly recommended that all SUTI communication using SUTI elements are encrypted.




                                                   Page 15 of 170
How to use SUTI


3. Establishing a SUTI Link
A common workflow for establishing a new SUTI Link can be described in brief as:

    1. A Client wishes to procure transport contracts with one or several Providers.
    2. The Client writes a Client Self Declaration, describing how they want to apply the SUTI standard.
    3. The Client procures the transport agreements, with the Client Self Declaration as basis for the
        communication of orders etc. between Client and Provider(s).
    4. Once an agreement has been signed, technical personnel from the Client and Provider:
             a. Fill in the Self Declaration Appendix - Technical documentation.xlsx (found in the SUTI standard
                 GitHub repo)
             b. Implement the Link Mapping messages (7100 & 7101)
             c. Implement the remaining messages based on the guidelines in the Client Self Declaration.

3.1 Self Declaration
A Client Self Declaration is the primary definition of what parts of the SUTI standard need to be implemented when
creating a SUTI Link for the specific Client. Providers create a simpler definition containing what parts of the SUTI
Standard is supported by the Provider system(s).

A Client Self Declaration shall contain a general and, over time, stable description of how the Client organization
uses SUTI to accomplish its missions.

The main target audience for a Client Self Declaration be Provider system manufacturers and developers. The
Client Self Declaration shall give enough information to make it possible to estimate the effort to develop an
implementation in compliance with the document.

An important purpose of the Client Self Declaration is also used as a requirement specification for communicating
with the Client system, that can be referred to in RFP or tender documents.

The SUTI technical committee is available to review and provide feedback on Client Self Declaration drafts. Good
practice is therefore to get feedback and final approval from the SUTI technical committee before publishing a new
Client Self Declaration.

3.2 Guidelines for Provider Self Declarations
Providers should normally limit their documentation to a specification of the types of SUTI Connections that are
supported. It should contain the following:

-   Types of connections supported
-   Examples of implemented SUTI Links, including who is Client and Provider of each Link.
-   Reference to Client Self Declarations including any restrictions

A Provider Self Documentation need not be reviewed by the SUTI technical committee, but Providers are
encouraged to make their declarations publicly available. SUTI provides a possibility to store Self Declarations in
the SUTI GitHub repository.

3.3 Guidelines for Client Self Declarations
The guidelines in this section outline how a Client Self Declaration should be structured. The chapters below are
therefore expected to be chapters and subchapters in a Client Self Declaration.

NOTE: There are numerous examples of Client Self Declarations from all major Clients in the Nordic market. It is
therefore advisable to read an example of a Self Declaration in parallel with this chapter.

3.3.1 Description of the organization
The Client should in a few paragraphs describe its organization. The description can be very short, e.g. just a
reference to the organization’s webpage URL.

3.3.2 Business model
Since SUTI is implemented where a Client acts as a representative for Travellers and Providers dispatch Drivers
and Vehicles that serve these Travellers, there will always be a business contract between the Client and the


                                                    Page 16 of 170
How to use SUTI

Provider. The terms of these contracts can vary a lot, from leasing a specific Vehicle for a time period, to
purchasing individual Trips on a spot market of Vehicle fleets.

Therefore, the Client Self Declaration should answer the following:

-   What characterizes the travellers and their general requirements?
-   How are trips that we send to Providers collected?
-   What type of business is done with Providers and how is their service requested?
-   How is that business supported? Are there mechanisms like logical Vehicles or other supporting tools?
-   What is the outline of a typical contract? (Not the legal document, just a few words describing the contract –
    who, where, when, scope of operation)
-   What software is used?

3.3.3 Flows
The Client Self Declaration should contain a flowchart for each type of Message Flow between the Client system
and the Provider system. For each Flow, state:

-   Objective of the Flow
-   Trigger of the Flow
-   Specific conditions at send time (if any) (not specific use of information elements, that will follow later)
-   Demands on action at Provider/Client
-   Demands on response
-   Handling if there is no response
-   Reference to related description in SUTI Use Cases
-   Any further comments vital to the Flow
-   Example of request and response

For easy overview, the chapter should start with a list of all messages that are exchanged. It is essential that all
Flows are documented.

3.3.4 Use of information elements
3.3.4.1 Documentation guidelines
There is no need to document all information elements in a Client Self Declaration. The examples in the Flows-
chapter should cover most of what is necessary. Instead, this chapter should focus on the essentials. See below
for examples of such essentials.

Requirements that are already documented in chapter 6 Examples of Self Declaration Flows do not need to be
documented in the Self Declaration. It is enough to simply define that you comply with the requirements.
Conversely, it is important to document any situations where your specification does not comply with the SUTI Use
cases.

SUTI can be used for several different types of operations, such as orders, repetitive orders, accounting and
questions and answers.

The following chapter contains a list of potential essential facts that should be documented for an order
application.

For a Client Self Declaration of an accounting application, the corresponding use case description in chapter 4.5
Accounting gives an extensive basis for such a documentation.

For a Client Self Declaration of repetitive orders some additional information elements will be required, that may be
necessary to comment. However, even for such a type of SUTI application you will find extensive basis for
documentation in the corresponding use case description in chapter 4.2 Repetitive Orders.

The documentation in this chapter will not necessarily need examples, since they generally can be found in the
Flow-examples. The documentation therefore consists mainly of plain text. However, exact naming of elements
and attributes as they are defined in SUTI schema should be used.

3.3.4.1   Documentation guidelines for an order application




                                                     Page 17 of 170
How to use SUTI

3.3.4.1.1 Use of id-elements
It is assumed that all implementations of SUTI comply with the rules for use of id-elements, documented in chapter
5.1.2 ID (Identification elements) Structures and Usage in SUTI.

The SUTI standard includes two messages – 7100 Link Mapping Request and 7101 Link Mapping Response, that
can be used to exchange the structure and source definitions for all id types. See chapter 3.5 Implementation of a
SUTI Link for more information.

3.3.4.1.2   Use of other elements
This section lists a number of questions, structured according to the SUTI Schema definition, that can be used in
the Client Self Declaration as a template to describe how SUTI elements are implemented.

The list can be copied and pasted into the Self Declaration as is, with answers to relevant questions added as
documentation. It is also possible to just answer a select number of questions that are relevant to the
implementation.

At msg-level
    - What will be the structure of orgReceiver?
    - Are there any referenceTo declarations in addition to the ones listed in chapter 4 Use cases (Flows)?
    - Does any of the Time stamps have a specific meaning?
At order-level
    - How are agreement and potentially idAgreement used?
    - Is Product used? If so what are the implications?
    - Are there any interpretations for process tags that are used and need to be highlighted?
At economy in order
    - Do we have price information? if so, is there a general implication or is the use dependent of agreement?
    - Do we use priceResponsible? If so, what do we expect from the Provider?
    - Do we use payment? If so, do we have specific use of e.g. idEkinfo or other element?
At resource in order
    - Have we transformed all requirements regarding all travellers into capacity and attributes, or will the
        Provider system have to do that? (not recommended)
    - Do we use id:s in any particular way?
    - Do we use vehicleStartPosition? If so, how shall the Provider system use this information (mandatory or
        guideline)?
At node level
    - What type of addresses will be handled?
    - How are addresses verified?
    - Do the addresses comply with SUTI Use Cases?
    - How is timesNode used and, specifically, what timeTypes are used
    - What type of content is transported?
    - Are there any essential content information elements to highlight?
    - Do we use any content identification?
    - Do we use connection? If so, how shall the Provider system use this information?
    - Are there specific demands for node information to be visible to the driver?
    - Do we use node process tags?
For order reject
    - How is order reject handled?
    - Which reasons for rejection will be accepted and how shall they be presented?
For order changes (the method of order change shall be reflected in a flow)
    - What will be valid reasons an for order change?
    - Will changes of order by the Provider be accepted?
    - How is the situation where a Provider for some reason cannot fulfill a started and accepted order?
For dispatch
    - What detailed requirements on vehicle or driver are there?
For submitting of vehicle positions
    - What types of format are supported?
    - How is the situation handled when coordinates are not available?
For events (4010)
    - Are there any critical events that need to be highlighted?
    - If we events are used, what timeTypes are requested?
For direct communication with driver or Provider traffic control operator
    - What are potential motives for these types of functions?
When a report and or delivery message is used (note that such flows must be present in the Flows section)
                                                   Page 18 of 170
How to use SUTI

    - What are the requirements for such messages?
For use of attributes to vehicle, driver or content
    - Include reference to a web-page containing actual list of attributes
    - Are there any custom attributes? How shall they be presented to the driver or otherwise be handled by the
        Provider
    - Are there other sources than the SUTI source (should be avoided)

3.3.5 Communication method
This section describes how the Provider should communicate with the Client. The documentation shall be very
precise. A link to a webpage where the documentation can be found is also acceptable.

3.3.6 Acceptance testing
The Client should document how acceptance testing should be performed. If there are test protocols that need to
be followed before deploying to production, there should be links to such information.

3.3.7 Contact information
The Client should provide contact information for the person responsible for the Self Declaration, in case the
reader needs additional information.

3.3.8 Document versioning
The Client Self Declaration shall have a version number, starting with 1.0 for the first official version. Minor
changes, e.g. textual changes that don’t affect the implementation, shall render a new minor version, e.g. 1.1, 1.2
etc. Major changes like adding a new flow, shall render a new major version, e.g. 2.0.

3.4 Specification of SUTI requirements in a Request for Proposal (RFP)
When the Client issues a Request for Proposal or Public procurement that includes a requirement for
communicating using the SUTI standard, the following shall be included:

    1. A Client Self Declaration, preferably as an appendix to the procurement documents or a reference to a
       publically available Client Self Declaration.
    2. A Self Declaration Appendix - Technical documentation Excel file that can be used by the Client and
       Provider to exchange technical information about the SUTI Link to be established.

Even though it may not affect the SUTI implementation itself, the following additional information can be useful in
clarifying the conditions if the future implementation. Note that when the term agreement is used below it refers to
the agreement that will be the result of the RFP.
.
     - Provider systems are generally very closely integrated with hardware and software in the vehicles. Any
         requirements for new hardware or new use of existing hardware (e.g. use of readers for new types of
         cards or other equipment) should be carefully documented. If this needs to be represented in the SUTI-
         communication, it should be clearly documented.
     - If the RFP requires specific actions by drivers in the car and these actions shall be handled by the
         Provider system, they need to be precisely documented.
     - If handling of economical information is described in the RFP, e.g. fines, it should be accompanied by an
         exact description how the information is transmitted using SUTI messages, including types of
         messages/events and the type of information used.
     - In case the RFP requires some essential information elements to be used in a very specific manner, it
         shall be documented in the RFP. This could for example be that an optional element shall be seen as
         mandatory, or an element/attribute is required to have specific values.
     - For any operation there will always be unexpected situations that have not been documented. The Self
         Declaration should describe a procedure for handling such situations.
     - A description of test and acceptance conditions before going live.
     - Specific requirements on the system communication.
     - A description of the technical organization for test and live operations of SUTI-links.

3.5 Implementation of a SUTI Link
After an agreement to use SUTI for communication based on a Client Self Declaration has been established
between a Client and a Provider, the implementation phase contains the following activities.


                                                    Page 19 of 170
How to use SUTI

3.5.1 Technical documentation
Technical personnel from the Client fills in the Self Declaration Appendix - Technical documentation.xlsx (a
template is found in the SUTI standard GitHub repo).

The technical documentation is then passed to the Provider, where technical personnel fill in the corresponding
information for the Provider.

A complete technical documentation contains information about the Agreement and its parties, reference to the
Client Self Declaration used as basis for the implementation and contact information for technical personnel
involved in the implementation. It also contains technical details about what communication protocol to use, SUTI
Link URL, if a VPN-connection needs to be established, how credentials are stored and exchanged, etc.

Each party is responsible for keeping the Technical documentation, including contact information for technical
personnel, updated for the entire life span of the Agreement and the corresponding SUTI Link implementation.

3.5.2 Link Mapping
The SUTI standard provides the messages 7100 Link Mapping Request and 7101 Link Mapping Response, that
outline the format of the different Id Types to be used by the SUTI Link being implemented. The 7100 and 7101
Link Mapping documents can either be provided:

-   as part of the SUTI communication interface, where a SUTI Link is initialized by the Client sending a msg 7100
     Link Mapping Request to the Provider, who responds with a 7101 Link Mapping Response.
-   as static XML-files exchanged between the Client and Provider or
-   pasted into the designated Excel-sheets in the Self Declaration Appendix - Technical documentation.xlsx
     template.

In the Link Mapping messages, the Client and Provider lists all Id Types and their respective formats. See chapter
5.1.2 ID (Identification elements) Structures and Usage in SUTI for more information about how to implement a
Link Mapping message. Note that the contact information in the Link Mapping XML needs to be kept up-to-date.

3.5.3 Implementation
Developers at the Provider and Client implement and test the remaining SUTI Messages, based on this official
documentation, the guidelines in the Client Self Declaration and the Technical documentation.




                                                   Page 20 of 170
How to use SUTI

4.1.1.4   Extensive flow with traffic control (Node by Node)

General

This flow can be used when the Client has an agreement that the Provider shall put resources available to the
Client. When the resource is available the Client will add work to each vehicle by providing more nodes. The Client
will end the process by a separate message (4020). The method for starting the process can differ but generally
the first node is always present in the initial order as is represented in this flow.
The Client must always accept the suggested resources before starting the process to pick-up first node.
As for other flows above additions of Order Events forms the process to a general order process. Normally this is
restricted to 2010 (Order cancellation request) prior to accept of resources (3002).

4.1.1.6     Order by Order

4.1.1.6.1 General
The order-by-order has developed as a method to send simple orders (base orders) and get them combined into a
driverSession. From SUTI 2017 there is a new method to do this by introduction of a whole new set of messages.
See chapter driverSession for more details.

However the method is already implemented for several links and is supported by all major Provider systems in
the Nordic countries. Therefore we have introduced this description to the standard.

The method is a way to impement traffic control for Client and use simple orders instead of the node-by-node
method. The Provider systems will always sort the nodes by estimated time. The way the orders are gathered into
a driverSession is by use of an idVehicle, which is the same for all orders in a driverSession.

In this chapter you will find an example where three orders are combined to one common routing. Recommended
solution.This example contains 3 travellers (Ada, Beda and Ceasar).

4.1.1.7     driverSession


4.1.1.7.1 General
The driverSession is a development of order-by-order where simple orders (base orders with no suborder) can be
added to a driverSession and when modified several orders can be modified simultaneously.

The driverSession is a type of ordermsg (2100) containing description of all resources for entire order. The order is
named driverSession to indicate that it may consist of several base orders that can be added from start or may be
added in a successively mode. They will all be performed in a continuous session using the same vehicle and
driver from first pickup of first order to last dropoff of last order, all in accordance with node-by-node and order-by-
order.

However with driverSession the Provider may implement communication to vehicle in which way the Provider
choose to do. To differentiate from node-by-node and order-by-order not all info must be communicated
immediately. The Provider system constructs a nodelist by sorting alla ordernodes by estimated time including
possible consideration of dwell time. The nodelist is sent to Client in all accept and reject messages as a response
to all updates of the session.

None of the major Provider systems have from 2017 the support to “keep” the base order information and
successively release to vehicle, although this may be a preffered situation. Theefore it I wise if the Client choose to
implement a solution close to order-by-order that has a support from the major Providersystems.

In order to make it possible for the Providersystem to develop a support for successive communication of nodes to
vehicle the Client system must provide the Provider not only with expected times.but also with information
regarding expected times at node. These times shall be presented in dwell time for the node (dwell time means
expected time from arrival at node to pickup or dropoff).

When such an implementation is done by a Providersystem it may accommodate different types of equipment in a
vehicle fleet. Until that happens the Providersystem may have problems communicating certain changes to
driverSessions.

For driverSession the dispatch will take place for the entire session. Thus msgs 3xxx will only be used for first msg
2100. Once the vehicle is accepted this will be the vehicle for all the orders inside the driverSession. With this also
follows the right for the Provider to reject updates of the driverSession wich is not inside the resources of the
chosen vehicle (and driver).

Observe that for the dispatch the Provider do not need to investigate the resurces of each order. Only the
resources of the session is vital for this purpose.

The Traffic control will be related to the nodes of each order and the sessions will be ended by the Client sending
a 4020 msg.

The development of the driverSession uses the same msg 2100 as the first initiation of the session. However the
dispatch will only take place once. Once the vehicle is accepted after msg 3003 no more dispatch msg shall be
sent. In all 2100 msg there are a changelog holding the instructions from Client for orders in the session. By using
the change-values added, updated, cancelled or moved the Client gives the instruction to add, update, cancel or
remove order from session. In case the order is moved it may (or may not) appear in other sessions.Such an order
cannot be performed without being placed into another driverSession. If it is cancelled it cannot be reffered to
again with its original orderid.

The reporting may take place for either each order or for the entire driverSession.

Below you will find a recommended solution for three orders (Ada, Beda and Caesar) in correspondence with
order-by-order above.

Recommended Solution

4.1.1.8     Order Trading

General

Order trading is in itself a fairly simple flow as described below. The dynamics of the flow is that it contains sending
of order to multiple Providers that may offer vehicles according to specifications given in the order. Ultimately one
of the orders will be accepted and all others cancelled.

When using the order trading, the start location tag can be used to give the Client a sophisticated opportunity to
choose “the closest” vehicle. The vehicle start position can be setup as following example.

<vehiclestartLocation typeOfCoordinate="WGS-84" lat=”58.39193” long=”13.42448” precision="10">
               <vehicleDistance range="1500">
                             <startTime>
                                          <time timeZone="1" time="2010-08-18T07:00:00"/>
                             </startTime>
                             <stopTime>
                                          <time timeZone="1" time="2010-08-18T07:00:30"/>
                             </stopTime>
               </vehicleDistance>
               <vehicleDistance range="5000">
                             <startTime>
                                          <time timeZone="1" time="2010-08-18T07:00:31"/>
                             </startTime>
                             <stopTime>
                                          <time timeZone="1" time="2010-08-18T07:01:00"/>
                             </stopTime>
               </vehicleDistance>
</vehiclestartLocation>

This allows the Provider that has a vehicle in a distance closer than 1500 meter radius to send a Msg 3000
between 07:00:00 and 07:00:30. If no vehicle is available no Msg 3000 can be sent. However between 07:00:31
and 07:01:00 Providers having a vehicle between 1500 and 5000 meter radius can send a Msg 3000.




                                                    Page 38 of 170
How to use SUTI

4.1.1.9   PreeOrder with preeOrder flag “PROVIDER”

General

PreeOrder is a preebooked order submitted to the Provider and cannot have the timetype as soon as possible.
The preebooked order can either be sent as a part of Msdg 2800 repetitive order or as a single order Msg 2000. In
both cases the process flag preeorder must be used. In the flow bellow Msg 2000 will be used. For Msg 2800 see
chapter 2.1.11.

In this case the Provider has the control and is the part that signals that no more alterations will be accepted by
sending Msg 2050 Order freeze to the Client.

4.1.2 Additional processes

4.1.2.1     Order status

General

This process is simple. The Client ask for the information the Provider has of a specified order and the Provider
sends this information to the Client. This process can be used for several reasons:
    - the Clients has not received appropriate messages in due time and need to secure its information status
        on an order
    - the Client has received a request from a traveller on a node and wants to secure its information status on
        an order
    - the Client has had a failure and wants to secure its information status on an order

Observe in process below the Provider sends a 2531 with the requested order information. There is an alternative
that the Provider sends 2532 in case the order is not registered by the Provider.

Recommended Solution

                                                                                response to the request
n+2                                           Sends MSG2531                     The message shall be
                                                                                sent without delay.
n+3         Receives MSG2531

4.1.2.2        Alteration in dispatch with dispatch reject

General

These described process alternatives comes into action when the Client must accept a suggested resource and
the first suggestion is not accepted by Client.
The Client must set the process flag dispatch=”true” and dispatchResponsible=”client” in its initial order to make
this process possible.
As seen below the Provider initiate by sending its proposed resources. The Client must answer immediately with
either msg 3002, as described in the flow-chapter, or with 3001 in case the suggested resource is not accepted.
If the answer is 3001, the Provider should send a new alternative suggestion with a new 3000.
This continues until the Client answers with a 3002 or the Provider has no more suggestions. In this case the
Provider should send 2005 Order reject request.

4.1.3 Order events Client

4.1.3.1   Order/node cancellation

Order/node cancelation can only be sent by the Client.

In the case of node cancelation it’s necessary to provide the node in referencesTo.

The different conditions that regulate the different answers shall be agreed upon in the agreement.


4.1.3.1.1 Order/node cancelation accept

In the simplest case a message 2010/2020 is sent from the Client to the Provider and the Provider accepts the
cancelation with a message 2011/2021 without any delay. The message need to have the attribute
cancelationAcceptance set to TRUE and cancelationConsequence set to FALSE.

In such a case the Client cannot send the same order with the same idOrder. Should the Client do so the Provider
should without any delay answer with 2003 orderreject and the rejectreason shall be 2513 ordercancelled.


4.1.3.1.2 Order/node cancellation accepted with consequence

This alternative shall be used if the Provider has started using resources that can be base for a claim on the Client.
The message need to have the attribute cancelationAcceptance set to TRUE and cancelationConsequence set to
TRUE. The accepted reasons for an acceptance with consequences shall be agreed upon in the agreement.

The economical consequence shall be stated in economycancellation


4.1.3.1.3 Order/node cancellation reject

This alternative shall be used if the Provider can’t cancel the order. Reason for this can be that the order has
already begun or the order is already finished. The accepted reasons for a rejection shall be agreed upon in the
agreement.

4.1.3.2   Order alteration

An accepted way to make an alteration of an already accepted order is as follows.

The Client sends a new updated order with the same idOrder as the old one. This order shall have a referencesTo
that includes the Providers idOrder. If the execution of the order hasn’t started the Provider just replaces the old
order with the new one. If the execution has started and a msg 4010 pickup has been sent for a node it’s
impossible to alter this node. In this case only future alterations will be accepted. This way of doing an alteration
give the possibility to do alterations after the execution of the order has started. If this way is used to handle an
alteration need to be agreed upon by both Client and Provider in the agreement. If the new altered order should
exceed some limit agreed upon in the agreement it’s impossible to use msg 2002 order reject as the Provider has
accepted the ownership of the order. Msg 2002 can only be used to reject the original order. After the acceptance
of the order the Provider has the responsibility for the order and must use msg 2005 order reject request after the
acceptance of the order. If this method is used the process flagg orderAlteration should be set to TRUE.

Another accepted way of doing an alteration is to send a msg 2010 order cancelation followed by a new order.
This order must not include any reference to the Providers old idOrder. In this case it’s not possible to make
alterations after the execution of the order has started.

To be discussed: The technical committee has a suggestion to handle the last case. If the Client want to alter an
order after the execution has started it could be possible to use a new set of messages, node reject request that is
built in the same way as order reject request. If the Client wants’ to alter a node in a order that has been started
the Client send a msg 2020 node cancelation and after the acceptance from the Provider the Client sends a msg
4001 that includes the new node information.


                                                    Page 50 of 170
How to use SUTI

The technical committee also suggests that addition of nodes to an existing order by sending a msg 4001 with new
node information without the Providers system sending a msg 4000 to request such information.




                                                 Page 51 of 170
How to use SUTI

4.1.3.3   Late order

A late order is an order that is sent from Client to Provider after it actually should have been performed or the
scheduled time is “impossible” to reach. .It’s necessary that this is regulated in the agreement.

In the SUTI standard the times on a node has the following timetypes. In the text below we use pickup but it’s
appropriate for other eventtypes.

 2105         scheduled               This is the scheduled time for the vehicle to be at the pickup address. This
                                      normally the same time as the time that the Traveller and Client have
                                      agreed upon to be the pickup time. Thus this is the latest time that the
                                      traveller expects the vehicle to be on the pickup address.
 2106         promised                In case where is a difference between the scheduled time and the time
                                      given to the Traveller the Client has the option to submit this agreed time by
                                      using 2106 promised. Both 2106 promised and 2105 scheduled must be
                                      included in the message in this case.
 2108         asap                    This is used for an immediate order where the Client and the Traveller has
                                      agreed that the vehicle shall be at the pickup address as soon as possible
                                      after the set time. Thus the set time could either be the booking time or a
                                      “best guess” from the Client and it is normally the earliest time that the
                                      vehicle shall be on the pickup address.
 2107         estimated               This is a value that can be used from either Client or Provider depending of
                                      the situation. The value means that has been an estimation of a new time
                                      for pickup. This is a “best guess” and not related to promise to the Traveller
                                      and/or agreement..
 2104         actual                  It’s the actual time of the event. This must be submitted by the Provider.
              Timetype not            In this case the default value is the same as 2104 actual e.g. in the
              specified               message block.

There are two ways to handle a late order. The way to handle these two ways shall be specified and agreed upon
in the agreement.


4.1.3.3.1 The hard way orderReject (Client is responsible for the order)

The Provider shall use IdRejectreason 2505 to late. The Client has the option to send the order back with the
same idOrder with appropriate alterations. An appropriate alteration should be to change the value of timetype
from scheduledtime to asaptime or send a new scheduledtime within an acceptable timeframe.


4.1.3.3.2 The softer way pickup confirmation (Provider is responsible for the order)

The Provider sends a message 4010 pickup confirmation. The message shall contain at least a
asap timetype with the value of time set to the earlier given scheduled time. And in the best of worlds the message
should also contain an estimated time.




                                                    Page 52 of 170
How to use SUTI

4.1.4 Order events Provider

4.1.4.1     Order reject

An order reject shall be sent without any delay and the reasons for an order reject shall be agreed upon in the
agreement and no order accept shall be sent. If the reasons for reject aren’t specified in the agreement every
order reject shall include a reject reason.

4.1.4.2     Order reject request

Order reject request can only be sent after that the Provider has accepted the order is within the agreement.

Sending the order reject request is done then the Provider wishes that should not to any part be handled by the
Provider. The reason for this can be lack of capacity. If this is the case the message should contain idrejectreason
2504 nocapacity. The Client should without no delay answer with any of the messages 2006 order reject request
accepted or 2007 order reject request rejected depending on the agreement situation.

                                                             Msg 2006 Client – Provider

There is a special case of order reject that may occur depending on agreement situation. This is handling of a
request from the Traveller to cancel the order made directly to Provider. This situation can occur when the
Provider has the authority to handle such requests. The procedure is the same as above but the idrejectreason
should be 2506 travellerrequest. The Provider must in both cases wait for the answer from the Client before setting
the order status to cancel.

4.1.4.3   Node events

All node events are normally initiated by driver. Therefore this type of information is always true in the sense that
the driver actually did the action. Errors depending on troubles with equipment is unlikely to occur.

Since long it has always been the question of quality of the messages. Is the vehicle really at the node? Is the
passenger actually droped off?

From 2017 SUTI has introduced two type of responses to the 4010 that is normally sent and is showed below.

The 4011 will be the normal response saying from Client that the event has been received without complaints. But
the Client may respond with 4012 saying that the event has been received but with complaints. The complaint will
be placed in a reject form that the Provider shall send to driver.

The last action of a node event is however the driver. If the passenger has been pickedup so is the truth, therefore
the driver may either change his action or repeat the action.

The driver may however repeat any 4010 message and last 4010 for an event type is the truth wether the Client
system has responded 4011 or 4012 (the event types are described in 3.2). If the driver has repeated the 4010 for
the eventtype he/she has all right to believe that the Client system shall register the event as true even if the
response is 4012.

All node events except the 1710 vehicle at node should always only be sent using actual time types that has
values very close to the actual time of day. The information type is of type real time and the value ot the
information is depending on the quality and and accuracy of the information.

With the 1710 an estimation of vehicle at node may be sent. Then it is obvious that it should contain estimated
times.



4.1.4.3.1 Message flow for node events

          Client                              Provider                              Comment

1                                             Sends MSG4010                         Shall include the node
                                                                                    number and a timetype
                                                                                    containing attribute 2107
                                                                                    estimatedtime.
2         Sends either msg 4011 or msg                                              In case 4012 ith should
          4012 after receipt of msg 4010                                            include reasons posiible
                                                                                    to send to vehicle and
                                                                                    driver
3                                             Sends msg 4010 again                  The information is either
                                                                                    changed due to errors in
                                                                                    last msg 4010 or
                                                                                    repeated
4         Sends either msg 4011 or msg                                              If the msg 4010 was
          4012 after receipt of msg 4010                                            repeated with the same
                                                                                    information the Client
                                                                                    should send 4011

4.1.4.4   Request for additional or corrected node information

If the information in a node in an order or a node in requested traffic information is incomplete it is possible to send
a msg 4000 to request for additional or corrected information for instance the need of information about accurate
positions or correct addresses. To request more information send a 4000 with idNode in referencesTo set to the
node in question.

Flow chart



                                                     Page 54 of 170
How to use SUTI




                                                               Provider
    Client
                           Msg 4000 Provider - Client
                  Msg 4000 including idNode in referencesTo.




                               Page 55 of 170
How to use SUTI

4.1.5 Order by Order and driverSession special processes
4.1.5.1 Resource description
Although orders are sent as besic orders the resource description at order level should contain the resource
description for the entire session and should be the same for all prders.And especially important in the first order
since this order guides the choice of vehicle for dispatch.

For driverSession this is much more defined since the driverSession holds the resource description necessary for
dispatch and the Providersystem can ignore the resources at each order.

4.1.5.2 Adding new order
For order by order the entire nodelist is always presented to driver in the vehicle equipment. It is likely that this will
also be true for the first implementations of driverSession. A new order that requires action from the driver should
be placed not later than as an immediate reaction (answer) to the message 4010 for previous node.

A certain preplacing of orders may be wise in order to secure that the driver always knows next step. Sending
uncertain preplanning long in advance has on the other hand been tested with poor result resulting in problems
communicating with vehicle and driver.

With driverSession when the Provider has developed the full responsibility for the communication with vehicle and
driver it may however be wise to send the whole planning of a session all the time.

In case the driver has started with intent to pickup or dropoff of a passenger, and an addition takes place of a new
order inside the session, the driver has to make a change of action. It is possible and may be a very good
planning. It is however wise to attend the driver by sending an extra message to vehicle to assure that the
message gets through. The action may result in a longer time and distance to next node than else would be
expected.

4.1.5.3 Change of orders
In the order by order changes is always sent either with order cancellation or resending the order with
orderAlteration=True or a combination of order cancellation and resending the order depending on the situation
and Provider system.

In driverSession this is much clearer defined. The following holds true for driverSession and is normally also true
for order by order.

1 No cancellation of order or move of order from session may take place after a 4010 has been sent for first node
of an order. The Provider system can reject this.

In case the vehicle has started its action to pickup a passenger and this is broken by a cancellation or move there
has obviously been a cost for this by the Provider.The solution for this will be dependant on the agreement. In the
reporting of a driverSession however the time and distance will be included. It is recommended to minimize this
type of action and reduce to very late cancellations by passengers.

If the 4010 for the first pickup is a true noshow the Client may cancel nodes in sequence after the first node inside
an order. It is done by sending an update of the order and only include one node. The entire order cannot be
cancelled or moved.

In case that the Client has contact with the passenger and “knows” that it should not be a true noshow, the Client
may send 4012 requsting the driver to search further. In case the driver then sends a pickup confirmation after a
successful pickup this will replace the noshow.

2 Update may only occur for nodes where successful 4010 has not been received.

3 Update of other nodes than first node of an order in a session may not concern content but is restricted to times
and geography. Problems with content shall be exchanged on first node in every order.

4 Update of an order in a session after 4010 dropoff of last node in the order is not acceptable and should result in
msg 2102 for such an update.




                                                      Page 56 of 170
How to use SUTI

   <!-- Reference to the message that this message answers. -->
   <idMsg src="PLANET_0001:MsgId" id="20131125NNNNNNNN" unique="true"/>
  </referencesTo>
  <orderTemplate>
   <idOrderTemplate src="HDSKOL_0001:TurIdNr" id="238" unique="true" orderTemplateName="A1"/>
   <orderTemplateCalendar/>
   <scheduleElements>
     <scheduleElement scheduleElementSequenceNbr="1">
      <scheduleElementReferencesTo>
        <idOrder src="HDSKOL_0001:HD_OrderNr" id="27641" unique="true"/>
   </scheduleElementReferencesTo>
   <scheduleElementRoute>
    <!-- nodeType="1803" => "pickup" -->
    <node nodeSeqno="1" nodeType="1803">
      <addressNode addressName="Frångatan 1" postalNo="12345">
       <manualDescriptionAddress sendtoInvoice="false" sendtoVehicle="true" sendtoOperator="true"
manualText="#H:08:00#Frångatan 1#PN: 12345#Adr.Anm" vehicleConfirmation="true"/>
       <attributeAddress>
         <attribute>
       <idAttribute src="HDSKOL_0001:AdrAnm" id="Adr.Anm" unique="false"/>
            </attribute>
       </attributeAddress>
      </addressNode>
      <timesNode>
       <!-- timeType="2101" => scheduledtime -->
       <time timeZone="1" time="2013-12-09T08:00:00" timeType="2101"/>
      </timesNode>
      <contents>
       <!-- contentType="1001" => traveller -->
       <content contentType="1001" name="Elev 1">

4.3 SUTI flagstops

På SUTI konferencen i februar 2010 og i september 2010 diskuterede vi de nye telegrammer 2900 Authorization
request og 2901 Authorization accept. Telegrammerne er beskrevet i dokumentet
V2_3_EconomicalTransactions.pdf.

Trapeze vil gerne anvende disse telegrammer til en flagstop logik, og det kræver at der tilføjes et content element
som beskriver kunden i Msg2900. Desuden er det nødvendigt med en ny subnodeType ved navn flagstop.

4.3.1 Flagstop forløb


En kunde kommer ind i en taxi og kører sit smartcard igennem en kortlæser i taxien. Kortnummeret sendes til et
Client system som svarer tilbage med info om kunden og accept for om kunden kan køre en tur. Info skal bestå af
kundens navn, adresse og saldo på kundens konto.
Hvis kunden ikke findes i Client systemet eller kunden ikke må køre, svares der tilbage med en afvisning og en
årsag til afvisningen.

Hvis kunden er godkendt til at køre en tur, samler taxien kunden op og sender de geografiske koordinater,
kundenr og smartcardId til Client systemet. Det svarer til at sende en enkelt node til Client systemet, og noden
udføres med det samme.
Når kunden senere sættes af, sendes node 2 som er kundens destination. I Client systemet kan de to noder nu
samles til en booking og prisen på turen kan bogføres på kundens konto.

4.3.2 Flagstops i SUTI

4.3.2.1   Authorization

Flagstop forløbet består af to dele. Den første del er en autorisation, hvor en kunde godkendes til at køre en tur.
Her kan vi anvende Msg2900 Authorization Request.

Indholdet i telegrammet skal være smartcardId (et kortnr). Det placeres under referencesTo i idAuthorization:

<referencesTo>
    <idAuthorization id="12345678" src="trapezesoftware_Skånetrafiken_0001:smartCardId"></idAuthorization>
</referencesTo>

Msg2900 besvares med Msg2901 Authorization Accept. Til dette telegram skal der tilføjes et content element
(SUTI/Msg/AuthorizationAccept/Content), så man kan angive en kundes navn og øvrige oplysninger som hører
hjemme under content. Kundens hjemmeadresse og saldoen på kundens konto kan angives under
manualDescriptionContent.

4.3.2.2   Bookingen

Den anden halvdel af flagstoppet er en booking som sammensættes af to nodes. Den første node dannes ud fra
den geografiske position, hvor kunden stiger ind i bilen, og den anden node er adressen, hvor kunden sættes af.
Pickup noden kan angives i order elementet i Msg2900 Authorization request, og i subnodeType angives det at
det drejer sig om et flagstop:

<node nodeSeqno="1" nodeType="pickup" subnodeType="flagstop">

Efter at bilen modtager en Authorization Accept, sendes en Msg4010 Event Confirmation som bekræfter at kunden
nu er i bilen og er klar til at køre.

Dropoff noden sendes fra bilen til Clientsystemet, når kunden stiger ud af bilen. Det er en Msg4010 Event
Confirmation hændelse igen med flagstop som subNodeType:

<pickupConfirmation eventType="passengerdropped">
   <nodeConfirmed nodeType="destination" nodeSeqno="2" subnodeType="flagstop">

Når Clientsystemet har modtaget flagstop destination noden, sender Clientsystemet Msg4020 End of order. Og
dermed er kommunikationen vedrørende flagstoppet slut.

                                                     Page 90 of 170
How to use SUTI



Hvis der i Msg2901 Authorization request er angivet deliveryNote under process elementet:

<process allowRouting="false" trafficControl="true" dispatchResponsible="client" report="false" preorderedVehicle="false"
dispatch="true" manualDispatch="false" deliveryNote="true"></process>

Så udvides flagstop kommunikationen med en Msg6500 deliveryNote og et tilhørende svar.

Se eksempler på telegrammer i filerne:

SUTI_Msg2900 Authorization request.xml
SUTI_Msg2901 Authorization Accept.xml
Msg4010_EventConfirmation_pickup.xml
Msg4010_EventConfirmation_destination.xml


Her er en skitse af message flow:


    Client                              Msg2900 Authorization request                                  Provider


                                        Msg2901 Authorization accept
         OR
                                        Msg2902 Authorization reject


                                        Msg4010 Event confirmation

                                        Msg4010 Event confirmation


                                        Msg4020 End of order


                                        Msg6500 Delivery note (optional)

                                        Msg6501 Delivery note accept
         OR
                                        Msg6502 Delivery note reject




                                                        Page 91 of 170
How to use SUTI


4.4 Delivery Note and associated messages

4.4.1 How to request a Msg 6500 Delivery Note

A Msg 6500 can be requested in the following 3 ways.

4.4.1.1   Request for Msg 6500 by using process element in a Msg 2000

A Msg 6500 can be requested by using the attribute deliveryNoteRequest in Msg 2000 Order in the process
element.

<process manualDispatch="false" dispatch="true" dispatchResponsible="provider" trafficControl="false"
report="false" preorderedVehicle="false" allowRouting="false" deliveryNoteRequest="true"/>

4.4.1.2   Request for Msg 6500 by using process element in a Msg 2901

A Msg 6500 can be requested by using the attribute deliveryNoteRequest in Msg 2901 Order Authorization accept
in the process element.

<process manualDispatch="false" dispatch="true" dispatchResponsible="provider" trafficControl="false"
report="false" preorderedVehicle="false" allowRouting="false" deliveryNoteRequest="true"/>

4.4.1.3   Request for Msg 6500 by sending a Msg 6510

A Msg 6500 can be requested by sending a MSG 6510 deliveryNoteRequest to the Provider.

<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<SUTI xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
xsi:noNamespaceSchemaLocation="SUTI_MessageXSD_2_4_X_Inprogress.xsd">
    <orgSender name="Skånetrafiken">
       <idOrg src="SUTI:idlink" id="trapezesoftware_Skånetrafiken_0003" unique="true"/>
    </orgSender>
    <orgReceiver name="TaxiBilSyd">
       <idOrg src="SUTI:idlink" id="Holmedal_TaxiBilSyd_0001" unique="true"/>
    </orgReceiver>
    <msg msgType="6510" msgName="deliveryNoteRequest">
    <idMsg src="Trapezesoftware_Skånetrafiken_0003:MsgId" id="2011011312345678" unique="true"/>
       <referencesTo>
          <idMsg src="Holmedal_TaxiBilSyd_0001:MSGID" id="2011011315096979" unique="true"/>
          <idVehicle src="Holmedal_TaxiBilSyd_0001:VehicleNumber" id="1808" unique="true"/>
       </referencesTo>
    </msg>
</SUTI>




                                                 Page 92 of 170
How to use SUTI

4.4.1.4   Responding to a Msg 6510 with a Msg 6511

If the Client requests a Msg 6500 by using a Msg 6510 the Provider has the option to answer by sending a Msg
6511 deliveryNoteRequestReject. A Msg 6511 shall be sent immediately after a Msg 6510 has been received.

<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<SUTI xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
xsi:noNamespaceSchemaLocation="SUTI_MessageXSD_2_4_X_Inprogress.xsd">
    <orgSender name="TaxiBilSyd">
       <idOrg src="SUTI:idlink" id="Holmedal_TaxiBilSyd_0001" unique="true"/>
    </orgSender>
    <orgReceiver name="Skånetrafiken">
       <idOrg src="SUTI:idlink" id="trapezesoftware_Skånetrafiken_0003" unique="true"/>
    </orgReceiver>
    <msg msgType="6511" msgName="deliveryNoteRequestReject">
       <idMsg src="Holmedal_TaxiBilSyd_0001:MSGID" id="2011011315096980" unique="true"/>
       <referencesTo>
          <idMsg src="rapezesoftware_Skånetrafiken_0003:MsgId" id="2011011312345678" unique="true"/>
          <idVehicle src="Holmedal_TaxiBilSyd_0001:VehicleNumber" id="1808" unique="true"/>
       </referencesTo>
    </msg>
</SUTI>

4.4.2 Sending a Msg 6500 Delivery Note

If requested a Msg 6500 shall be sent within 1 business day. This time limit can be altered if a mutual
agreement between Client and Provider exists.
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<SUTI xmlns:xsi="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance"
xsi:noNamespaceSchemaLocation="SUTI_MessageXSD_2_4_X_Inprogress.xsd">
    <orgSender name="TaxiBilSyd">
       <idOrg src="SUTI:idlink" id="Holmedal_TaxiBilSyd_0001" unique="true"/>
    </orgSender>
    <orgReceiver name="Skånetrafiken">
       <idOrg src="SUTI:idlink" id="trapezesoftware_Skånetrafiken_0003" unique="true"/>
    </orgReceiver>
    <msg msgType="6500" msgName="deliveryNote">
       <idMsg src="Holmedal_TaxiBilSyd_0001:MSGID" id="2011011315096979" unique="true"/>
       <deliveryNote>
          <idReceipt id="12345" src="trapezesoftware_Skånetrafiken_0003:FakturaNr"></idReceipt>
          <eventReportDeliveryNote>
          <event eventType="pickup">
          <subOrderEvent>
             <idOrder id="12345" src="trapezesoftware_Skånetrafiken_0003:BookingId"></idOrder>
          </subOrderEvent>
          <eventContent contentType="companion" name="unknown">
          <resourceContent>
             <vehicle>
                   <capacity>
                       <seats noOfSeats="2" noOfItems="2"></seats>
                   </capacity>
             </vehicle>
          </resourceContent>
          </eventContent>
          </event>
          </eventReportDeliveryNote>
          <summaryReportDeliveryNote orderEnded="true" ></summaryReportDeliveryNote>
          <economyReportDeliveryNote>
<!--Commentary: <economyReportDeliveryNote> WITHOUT nodeSeqno means that this is reported TOTAL-->
             <formOfPayment>
                <payment paymentType="account" amount="125.00">
                   <idCurrency src="ISO 4217" id="SEK" unique="false"/>

                                                 Page 93 of 170
How to use SUTI

                    <vatAmountSpecification>
                       <totalVatAmount amountVat="25" amountInclVat="125" distance="23" duration="32">
                          <idAmount id="Total" src="Holmedal_TaxiBilSyd_0001:AmountId"></idAmount>
                       </totalVatAmount>
                    </vatAmountSpecification>
                  </payment>
                  <payment paymentType="socialservicefee" amount="50"></payment>
              </formOfPayment>
          </economyReportDeliveryNote>
          <economyReportDeliveryNote nodeSeqno="2">
<!--Commentary: <economyReportDeliveryNote> WITH nodeSeqno means that this is presented a NOD. Note!
Accounting should be done in the node where the traveller leaves the vehicle -->

                    <idCurrency src="ISO 4217" id="SEK" unique="false"/>
                  </payment>
